}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.79152pt}{-40.20557pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

that vertically imply that
l_{i}x_{1}=l_{i}
and
l_{i}x_{2}=l_{i}
for all
i\in\mathcal{I}
, thus
x_{1}=\mathrm{id}_{X}
and
x_{2}=\mathrm{id}_{X}
.
To then show the associativity and unit axioms for
(L,\mu,\eta)
, it suffices to compose with the also jointly cancellable
\lambda_{i}
’s and use the corresponding axioms for each monad
(D_{i},\mu_{i},\eta_{i})
and that
\lambda_{i}
’s are algebra maps:

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\mu(\mu\odot 1)

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})(\mu\odot 1)=\mu_{i}(\mu_{i}\odot 1)(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})=\mu_{i}(1\odot\mu_{i})(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})(1\odot\mu)=\lambda_{i}\mu(1\odot\mu)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\mu(1\odot\eta)

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})(1\odot\eta)=\lambda_{i}=\mu_{i}(\lambda_{i}\odot\lambda_{i})(\eta\odot 1)=\lambda_{i}\mu(\eta\odot 1)

where we suppressed horizontal composition associativity and unitality constraints.

Now we turn to filtered colimits. Once again, consider a diagram
D\colon\mathcal{I}\to\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})

with
\mathcal{I}
filtered, and take its colimit in
\mathsf{Alg}(F)

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q.}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Di}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\Downarrow\gamma_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

Since
F
preserves filtered colimits, again by standard endofunctor algebras facts we know that such colimits in
\mathsf{Alg}(F)
are created by functor
\mathsf{Alg}(F)\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
(
[
Por08
]
),
and using
2.21
and parallel colimits of
\mathbb{D}
, the vertical components
(q_{i}:X_{i}\to Q)
again form a colimiting cocone in
\mathbb{D}_{0}
, and in particular form a jointly epimorphic family.

To show that
(C,\mu,\eta)
is in
\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})
, one can use similar arguments as the ones above: first of all, the structure 2-cells of the colimit
(C,\mu,\eta)
are globular like before.
For the associativity and unit axioms for
(C,\mu,\eta)
, one can use a somewhat dual argument to the limit case, where in addition now we note that by assumptions,
composing the colimiting cocone
Eq.

8
horizontally with itself yields another colimit. In particular, the families of 2-cells
\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i}
are jointly epimorphic. So for example, associativity can be deduced from the following calculation

\displaystyle\mu(\mu\odot 1)(\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i})

\displaystyle=\mu(\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i})(\mu_{i}\odot 1)=\gamma_{i}\mu_{i}(\mu_{i}\odot 1)=\gamma_{i}\mu_{i}(1\odot\mu_{i})=\mu(\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i})(1\odot\mu_{i})

\displaystyle=\mu(1\odot\mu)(\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i}\odot\gamma_{i}).

∎

Remark 3.17
.

We should note here that the assumption of preservation of filtered colimits by all functors
-\odot M
and
M\odot-
, which is included in
2.26
of a locally presentable double category, is not in fact needed in the above proof. Rather, for the purposes of this result, these conditions are substituted with the corresponding ones on
1\colon\mathbb{D}_{0}\to\mathbb{D}_{1}
and
-\odot-\colon\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
, giving an example of what was discussed in
2.30
.

Example 3.18
.

The above assumptions hold in
\mathbb{D}=\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathbb{M}\mathsf{at}
, for
\mathcal{V}
a locally presentable monoidal category such that
\otimes
preserves (coproducts and) filtered colimits in each variable. First of all,
\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathbb{M}\mathsf{at}
is then locally presentable by
2.28
. Moreover, the preservation of filtered colimits by
-\odot-\colon\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Grph}\to\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Grph}
is essentially
[
KL01
, Lem. 3.2]
, while on can verify that
1\colon\mathsf{Set}\to\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathbb{M}\mathsf{at}_{1}
preserves all connected colimits.

We thus deduce that, under the stated assumptions on
\mathcal{V}
, the category of enriched categories
\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Cat}
is locally presentable.
In particular, this recovers
[
KL01
, Thm. 4.5]
.

Example 3.19
.

Similarly, one can see that the result also applies to
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{S}\mathsf{pan}(\mathcal{C})
, for
\mathcal{C}
a locally presentable category. By
2.28
,
\mathbb{S}\mathsf{pan}(\mathcal{C})
is locally presentable. The preservation of filtered colimits by the functor
1
is immediate, while the corresponding property for
-\odot-\colon\mathsf{Grph}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathsf{Grph}(\mathcal{C})
boils down to the commutativity of pullbacks and filtered colimits in
\mathcal{C}
. In this case, we deduce that
\mathcal{C}
being locally presentable implies the same for
\mathsf{Cat}(\mathcal{C})
, the category of internal categories in
\mathcal{C}
, see e.g.
[
Str80
, Thm. 3.24]
.

4.
Comonads

We now turn to the category
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
of comonads and comonad maps in double categories, recalled in
2.31
. It is known that even in the one-object case, namely comonoids in monoidal categories, the existence of cofree coalgebras is more demanding than its dual problem: for example, there does not exist a formula like the “geometric series” one for free monoids, used in the previous section. Inspired by Porst’s approach
[
Por08
]
concerning the existence of a right adjoint to
\mathsf{Comon}(\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{V}
for a monoidal category
\mathcal{V}
, we establish the existence of cofree comonads in double categories, dually to
3.2
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{D}
is a locally presentable double category. Then the forgetful functor
U\colon\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
has a right adjoint.

Proof.

The forgetful
U\colon\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
constitutes an opfibred 1-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

between the opfibrations
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathbb{D}_{0}
and
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathbb{D}_{0}
, see
2.37
, since cocartesian liftings are essentially the same.
Thus by the dual of
2.14
,
U
has a right adjoint when for every object
X\in\mathbb{D}_{0}
, the restrictions

U_{X}\colon\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})_{X}\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})_{X}
to the fibers have a right adjoint.

Since
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})_{X}=\mathbb{D}[{X},{X}]
and
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})_{X}=\mathsf{Comon}(\mathbb{D}[{X},{X}])
, this reduces to the cofree comonoid existence on the monoidal category
(\mathbb{D}[{X},{X}],\odot,1_{X})
. Since
\mathbb{D}
is locally presentable,

\mathbb{D}[{X},{X}]
is locally presentable by
2.29
,
and its tensor product
\odot
preserves filtered colimits in each
variable by
2.27
. Therefore by
[
Por08
, § 2.7]
,
U_{X}
has a right adjoint.
∎

As for the dual case of monads in double categories (
3.7
), it is the case that comonads are comonadic over endomaps as soon as cofree comonads exist, hence we obtain the following result.

Theorem 4.2
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{D}
is a double category such that
U\colon\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
has a right adjoint. Then
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
is comonadic over
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
.

In particular, combining the last two results yields the following.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a locally presentable double category. Then
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
is comonadic over
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
.

Finally, we establish conditions on
\mathbb{D}
under which the category of comonads
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
is locally presentable, namely the dual of
3.16
but with a different proof strategy. This again generalizes the fact that the category of comonoids in a locally presentable monoidal category
\mathcal{V}
is itself locally presentable
[
Por08
, § 2.7]
. The current proof adopts a similar, but eventually more involved technique, expressing the category of comonads as an equifier of certain natural transformations between accessible functors and then a limit theorem.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a locally presentable double category where
-\odot-\colon\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
and
1\colon\mathbb{D}_{0}\to\mathbb{D}_{1}
preserve filtered colimits, and furthermore suppose that
\mathbb{D}
has stable local initial objects
5
5

5

As will become clear from the proof, only functors of the form
\widecheck{g}\odot\textrm{-}\odot\widehat{f}
need to preserve initial objects. We note that this does not follow, since due to fibrancy, such functors have a
left
adjoint – analogously to
Eq.

5
.
. Then the category
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
of comonads is locally presentable.

Proof.

There is an endofunctor on the category of endomaps (which has products by
2.21
)

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

and morphisms in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
respect these structure maps.
This
F
-coalgebras category is locally presentable again by standard facts about endofunctor coalgebras, since
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
is locally presentable by
2.29
and
F
is finitary: both
-\odot-
and
1
preserve filtered colimits by our assumptions and therefore so does
F
as finite limits commute with filtered colimits in any locally presentable category.

Similarly to the proof of
3.16
, this category contains the category of comonads
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
: on the one hand the structure 2-cells
c_{1},c_{2}
are not globular, and on the other hand there are no coassociativity nor counitality axioms present. To this end, we consider the full subcategory
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
of
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
spanned by objects with globular structure maps, namely of the form

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\Downarrow\delta}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\Downarrow{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{X}}

This intermediate category is important, because the category of comonads in
\mathbb{D}
shall be expressed as an equifier category of natural transformations (expressing the comonad axioms) between functors with domain
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
which will in the end imply local presentability.
Thus, before describing the relevant transformations, we need to verify that
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
is also locally presentable.

First of all, it can be verified that
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
is accessibly embedded in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
– and is in fact closed under all colimits, thus cocomplete. We will now show that it is also closed under all subobjects and thus, by a remark under
[
AR94
, Cor. 2.36]
, accessible and therefore locally presentable. This requires the following lemma,
whose proof we postpone so as to not interrupt the main argument, by also noting that here is where theorem’s assumption regarding stable local initial objects is only used.

Lemma 4.5
.

Under the assumptions of
4.4
, any monomorphism
\alpha_{f}\colon C_{X}\to D_{Y}
in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
has the property that its source/target vertical map
f\colon X\to Y
is a monomorphism in
\mathbb{D}_{0}
.

In order to show the required closedness under subobjects, we now consider an object
C^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
and a monomorphism
\alpha\colon A\to C^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
, aiming to show that in fact
A\in\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
. Since
\alpha
is an
F
-coalgebra map, we have
that

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

and since
\alpha
is a monomorphism in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
, the above lemma implies that
f
is a monomorphism in
\mathbb{D}_{0}
. Hence from the left-hand side equation for the boundaries we get that
fz_{1}=f
and so
z_{1}=\mathrm{id}_{Z}
, and from the right-hand side equation for the boundaries we get that
fz_{2}=f
and so
z_{2}=\mathrm{id}_{Z}
. As a result, the
F
-coalgebra
A
has globular structure maps, hence
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
is closed under subobjects in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
.

We can now express
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
as an equifier of natural transformations between finitary functors emanating from the locally presentable
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}
as follows: if
U
is the forgetful functor, define

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\scriptstyle\Downarrow\phi_{1},\psi_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\scriptstyle\Downarrow\phi_{2},\psi_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\scriptstyle\Downarrow\phi_{3},\psi_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\odot^{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\odot 1_{(-)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{(-)}\odot-}

where

(\phi_{1})_{C}=(\delta*\mathrm{id}_{C})\circ\delta:C\to(C\odot C)\odot C\ \ \text{and}\ \ (\psi_{1})_{C}=(\mathrm{id}_{C}*\delta)\circ\delta:C\to C\odot(C\odot C)

will be used to force coassociativity,

(\phi_{2})_{C}=(\epsilon*\mathrm{id}_{C})\circ\delta:C\to 1_{\mathfrak{s}(C)}\odot C\ \ \text{and}\ \ (\psi_{2})_{C}=\ell_{C}:C\to 1_{\mathfrak{s}(C)}\odot C

for left counitality, and similarly

(\phi_{3})_{C}=(\mathrm{id}_{C}*\epsilon)\circ\delta:C\to C\odot 1_{\mathfrak{t}(C)}\ \ \text{and}\ \ (\psi_{3})_{C}=r_{C}:C\to C\odot 1_{\mathfrak{t}(C)}

for right counitality. The equifier of these natural transformations, namely the subcategory spanned by the objects
C
such that
(\phi_{i})_{C}=(\psi_{i})_{C}
for all
i=1,2,3
, is precisely
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
, and all categories and functors involved are accessible by our assumptions and constructions. In particular, the forgetful functor preserves all (filtered) colimits because it is the composite
\overline{\mathsf{Coalg}(F)}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\hookrightarrow\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
where both arrows in fact create all colimits – the first one as seen above and the second one due as is the case for any endofunctor coalgebras category. Hence by
[
AR94
, Lem. 2.76]
,
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
is an accessible category.

Finally,
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
is cocomplete by
2.38
and is therefore locally presentable as required.
∎

We now return to the proof of the earlier lemma. Before showing the claim, let us discuss its meaning. Recall that although colimits are created by the forgetful
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
for a
category of coalgebras for any endofunctor
F\colon\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
, limits in categories of coalgebras are in general not preserved by that forgetful functor. One case this
is true (e.g. dual of
[
AP03
, Prop. 4.6]
) is when
F
preserves said limits; since
F
in this case is given by
Eq.

9
, and horizontal composition is not expected to preserve e.g. pullbacks, monomorphisms are not in general preserved by
F
.
However, we can at least verify that the
vertical
part of a monorphism in
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
is in fact a
\mathbb{D}_{0}
-monomorphism,
which turns out to be precisely what was needed in the above proof.

Proof of
4.5
.

We will show that
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\to\mathbb{D}_{0}
preserves monomorphisms. In order to accomplish this, it will be convenient to factor this functor as a composition
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\to\mathsf{Coalg}(P)\to\mathbb{D}_{0}
, where
P
is the endofunctor

P\colon\mathbb{D}_{0}\xrightarrow{\phantom{AA}}\mathbb{D}_{0}\phantom{aa}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mapsto}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times f}

and
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\to\mathsf{Coalg}(P)
is defined by mapping
(C,c_{1},c_{2})\mapsto(X,(x_{1},x_{2})\colon X\to X\times X)
, namely for an
F
-coalgebra
C
as in
Eq.

10
this functor discards the structure 2-maps and only keeps track of their vertical boundary maps, which can then be grouped as a single map into a product. Since
P
clearly preserves pullbacks, the forgetful
\mathsf{Coalg}(P)\to\mathbb{D}_{0}
creates pullbacks and hence in particular preserves monomorphisms. So it suffices to show that
\mathsf{Coalg}(F)\to\mathsf{Coalg}(P)
also has this property. For the latter, we now show that this functor – call it
U
– has a left adjoint
L\colon\mathsf{Coalg}(P)\to\mathsf{Coalg}(F)
.

Let us denote by
I_{Z}
the initial object of
\mathbb{D}[{Z},{Z}]
for any
Z\in\mathbb{D}_{0}
. The fundamental observation we will make use of is the following: if vertical morphisms
f\colon Z\to X
and
g\colon Z\to Y
have been chosen, then there is a uniquely determined 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{Z}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}

for any
M\colon X\hbox to38pt{\vbox to9.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 18.99997pt\lower-2.46147pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

. The reason for this is that, because

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

\scriptstyle\bullet

\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widecheck{g}}

which then by fibrancy of

uniquely corresponds to a non-globular one of the previous form.

Now given a

-coalgebra
(X,x_{1},x_{2})
, we define

to be the

-coalgebra whose underlying 1-cell is

, with structure 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}}

the ones uniquely determined by their vertical components, as described above. Similarly, given a morphism
f\colon(X,x_{1},x_{2})\to(Y,y_{1},y_{2})
of
P
-coalgebras, we define
Lf\colon L(X,x_{1},x_{2})\to L(Y,y_{1},y_{2})
to be the uniquely determined 2-cell
I_{X}\Rightarrow I_{Y}
with vertical components both equal to
f\colon X\to Y
. The fact that this is indeed an
F
-coalgebra morphism follows once more from the fact that morphisms beginning from
I_{X}
are uniquely determined by their vertical components, where the desired equalities hold by the assumption that we have a
P
-coalgebra morphism to begin with. The functoriality also follows by uniqueness.

Finally consider an
F
-coalgebra morphism
L(Z,z_{1},z_{2})\Rightarrow(C,c_{1},c_{2})
as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\Downarrow}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle\bullet
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

where
(C,c_{1},c_{2})
is as in (
10
). Once again by the property of
I_{Z}
, the equalities making this 2-cell a morphism of
F
-coalgebras are valid iff they are so vertically, i.e. iff the pair of equalities
hz_{1}=x_{1}h
,
hz_{2}=x_{2}h
hold. But the latter is precisely the statement that
h\colon Z\to X
is a morphism of
P
-coalgebras. This shows that there is a bijection between
F
-coalgebra morphisms
L(Z,z_{1},z_{2})\Rightarrow(C,c_{1},c_{2})
and
P
-coalgebra morphisms
(Z,z_{1},z_{2})\to U(C,c_{1},c_{2})
, which by similar arguments is easily seen to be natural.

∎

Remark 4.6
.

Since
\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathbb{M}\mathsf{at}
is a locally presentable double category under the conditions of
2.28
, and in fact all colimits are preserved by functors
M\circ\textrm{-}\circ N
, we obtain that the category of cocategories
\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Cocat}
(
2.34
) is a locally presentable category itself. In fact, the above arguments can be used to fix a gap in the proof of
[
Vas19
, Prop. 4.31]
in the specific context of matrices.

Moreover,
4.5
applied in
\mathcal{V}\textrm{-}\mathbb{M}\mathsf{at}
says that any monomorphic
\mathcal{V}
-cofunctor comprises of a monomorphism between the set of objects of
\mathcal{V}
-cocategories, namely is injective
on objects. As an idea, this appears as the dual of the well-known fact that epimorphic functors are surjective on objects (but not surjective on arrows).

Remark 4.7
.

Using local presentability of comonads in a double category that satisfies the assumptions of
4.4
, one could re-prove the first part of the main result
[
ASV24
, Thm. 3.4.12]
in a more direct -- yet slightly less informative
6
6

6

In loc.cit., we obtain also the carrier object of the enriched homs as extra information.
– way. In more detail, if
\mathbb{D}
is a braided monoidal closed and locally presentable double category, we obtain a (tensored and cotensored) enrichment of
\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})
in
\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})
as follows. We first form the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\textrm{-},B)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\textrm{-},B)^{\mathrm{op}}}

where
H\colon\mathsf{Cmd}(\mathbb{D})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})\to\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathbb{D})
is the “internal-hom” functor, coming from the monoidal closed structure of the double category (
[
ASV24
, § 3.2]
) and restricts to the categories of monads and comonads exactly like convolution.

Then by
3.8
and
4.3
, both legs are comonadic by therefore create colimits, and also the bottom functor preserves colimits since it has a right adjoint as
\mathsf{End}(\mathbb{D})
is monoidal closed (
[
ASV24
, Rem. 3.2.11]
). Thus the top functor preserves colimits, and by a standard adjointness result (
[
Kel05
, Thm. 5.33]
), every cocontinuous functor from a locally presentable category has a right adjoint, here
H(\textrm{-},B)^{\mathrm{op}}\dashv P(\textrm{-},B)
. This implies the desired enrichment of monads in comonads, using a standard closed-action induced enrichment result originating back to
[
GP97
]
.
